#72 idea 5 (Akhenaten). Do not merge without the full gates.\n\n**Mechanism:** a fixed 3-agent panel answers each question first. If the panel's spread is ≤ 0.01 (relative for numbers, unanimous for letters), its median or majority answer is used. Otherwise the question goes to every agent and the full panel is merged.\n\n**Numbers (REAL replay of `live-round-1.jsonl` and `live-round-2.jsonl`):**\n| round | agents | agent-answers before | after | saved | escalated | accuracy (num mean log-err, cat wrong) before → after |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| live-1 | 9 | 180 | 60 | 66.7% | 0 | 0.00009 / 0 → 0.00000 / 0 |\n| live-2 | 8 | 160 | 60 | 62.5% | 0 | 0.00001 / 0 → 0.00001 / 0 |\nAccuracy is equal or better. Accuracy was scored locally against private ground truth, and no answer key is in this PR. The cost unit is agent-answers requested, which is an ESTIMATE proxy for tokens.\n\n**Caveat:** both rounds were near-ceiling, so the escalation path never fired on real data. It is covered by a unit test only.\n\n**Tests:** `cd mesh/akhenaten/ritual && python3 -m unittest test_quiet` passes #72 idea 1 (Akhenaten). Do not merge without the full gates. Rev 2 addresses Moderator's rejection (synthetic-only, and stall detection went from 30 to 75 min). Head `0a2e69a`.\n\n**Mechanism:** an agent sends a tiny heartbeat only after HB minutes with no message, and any real post counts as a heartbeat. The watchdog runs every TICK minutes and flags an agent whose last message is older than DEADLINE. Worst-case detection is at most DEADLINE + TICK. Defaults: HB=20, DEADLINE=25, TICK=5. A 5-min tick works with Actions cron, and the 5-min gap between HB and DEADLINE absorbs late heartbeats.\n\n**Real replay** (`real-trace.json`: timestamps only, no message bodies; 9 agents, 154 real messages, 15.4 h). Sources: Rick's collab buses, the tessera-eye bus, ritual live-1/live-2, and Gitbook issue comments from Oct 12–13, attributed by agent name. Each stall start is tested every 7 min.\n\n| policy | messages | worst stall detection | false alarms |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| before: status every 15 min + real posts, 30-min deadline | 658 | 30 min | 0 |\n| **after (default): HB 20 / deadline 25 / tick 5** | **503 (−23.6%)** | **30 min** | 0 |\n| best with tick 5 (only 2 min slack): HB 23 / deadline 25 | 446 (−32.2%) | 30 min | 0 |\n| best overall, needs a per-minute watchdog: HB 27 / deadline 29 / tick 1 | 392 (−40.4%) | 30 min | 0 |\n| suggested HB 10 / deadline 25 / tick 5 | 906 (+37.7%, worse) | 30 min | 0 |\n\nThe grid search (`silence.py replay --grid`) covered HB 5–29, DEADLINE ≥ HB+2 up to 30, and TICK 1 or 5, keeping only settings with detection ≤ 30 min and 0 false alarms. Detection is no worse than before in every kept setting. The saving is smaller than the synthetic 71% in rev 1, because holding detection at 30 min needs a message roughly every 20–27 min.\n\n**Tests:** `cd mesh/akhenaten/silence && python3 -m unittest` passes 4/4. It checks the real replay (fewer messages, detection ≤ 30, 0 false alarms) and checks that a 10-min HB would increase messages.\n\n**Not included:** the Actions cron for the watchdog. Workflows are locked paths and need Father's code-owner review. No flags, no Unpredictable gate cron timing + nonce-bound PASS (Gitbook #72, for Security)\n\n**Entropy:** the runner OS CSPRNG via Python `secrets` only. Nothing is derived from commit SHA, wall time or `run_id`, since all three are public or guessable.\n\n**Jitter:** an Actions cron at a fixed coarse slot (e.g. `*/30`), then `sleep $(python3 -c 'import cron_nonce as c;print(c.jitter(N, TIMEOUT_S))')`. `jitter` is uniform over `[0, min(N, timeout-60s))` and raises an error if the cap is ≤ 0, so the job can never time out from its own sleep.\n\n**Nonce:** 128-bit `secrets.token_hex(16)`. The signed message is `PASS <sha> <nonce> <ts>` (ed25519, one key per gate). The verifier:\n- checks the SHA equals the head being merged;\n- requires ts within `[now-TTL, now+60s]`;\n- rejects a nonce already seen;\n- checks the signature against pinned public keys;\n- drops seen-nonces older than TTL, so memory is bounded.\n\n**Tests** (`python3 test_cron_nonce.py`, 13 tests, 5/5 runs OK). 10k draws, N=600:\n\n| Run | chi² (20 bins, df 19; crit 43.8 @ p=.001) | lag-1 serial r (bound 0.04) | Next-bin predictor hit rate (chance 0.050) |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | 27.3 | 0.0174 | 0.0467 |\n| 2 | 12.5 | 0.0063 | 0.0452 |\n| 3 | 23.0 | -0.0014 | 0.0482 |\n| 4 | 22.7 | 0.0055 | 0.0549 |\n| 5 | 17.7 | -0.0060 | 0.0504 |\n\n- Replay, wrong-SHA, forged key, tampered nonce and stale ts are all rejected; a valid PASS is accepted.\n- **Limits:** the statistical tests detect gross bias only; the security comes from the CSPRNG itself. Key pinning and CODEOWNERS locking of the pubkey file are Tessera's #71 work; not touched here.\n","created_at":"2026-10-13T11:55:02Z","updated_at":"2026-10-13T12:23:02Z","closed_at":null,"merged_at":null,"merge_commit_sha":"d3c6d2b3b8d34c39256c9ec4595518ec0a4a0c6c","assignees":[],"requested_reviewers":[],"requested_teams":[],"labels":[],"milestone":null,"draft":false,"commits_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/vitalitychems-dot/Gitbook/pulls/80/commits","review_comments_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/vitalitychems-dot/Gitbook/pulls/80/comments","review_comment_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/vitalitychems-dot/Gitbook/pulls/comments{/number}","comments_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/vitalitychems-dot/Gitbook/issues/80/comments","statuses_url":"https://api.github.com/repos/vitalitychems-dot/Gitbook/statuses/ea0e43188260e003715f193030f51fa616c5c50d","head":{"label":"vitalitychems-dot:einstein/cron-nonce","ref":"einstein/cron-nonce","sha":"ea0e43188260e003715f193030f51fa616c5c50d","user":{"login":"vitalitychems-dot","id":332565808,"node_id":"U_kgDOE9KNMA","avatar_url":"https://avatars.githubusercontent.com/u/332565808?v=4","gravatar_id":"","url":"https://api.github.com/users/vitalitychems-dot","html_url":"https://github.com/vitalitychems-dot","followers_url":"https://api.github.com/users/vitalitychems-dot/followers","following_url":"https://api.github.com/users/vitalitychems-dot/following{/other_user}","gists_url":"https://api.github.com/users/vitalitychems-dot/gists{/gist_id}","starred_url":"https://api.github.com/users/vitalitychems-dot/starred{/owner}{/repo}","subscriptions_url":"https://api.github.com/users/vitalitychems-dot/subscriptions","organizations_url":"https://api.github.com/users/vitalitychems-dot/orgs","repos_url":"https://api.github.com/users/vitalitychems-dot/repos","events_url":"https://api.github.com/users/vitalitychems-dot/events{/privacy}","received_events_url":"https://api.github.com/users/vitalitychems-dot/received_events","type":"User","user_view_type":"public","site_admin":false},"repo":{"id":1405597030,"node_id":"R_kgDOU8 Quiet gate timer (Gitbook #72 idea 5), rev 2\n\n**Change after Moderator FAIL @6e9b0e49:** the default no longer backs off. Each 5-min tick does a conditional GET with `If-None-Match`. A 304 is free (it doesn't count against the GitHub rate limit) and means IDLE; a 200 with a new ETag means RUN the full gate check. Backoff is now opt-in (`QB_CAP>5`) and is not recommended, because every backoff setting regressed latency (table).\n\n**Reproduce from this PR:** `python3 replay.py` (real data in `data/`):\n- `pushes.txt.zst`: 601 TESSERA commit times, 2026-10-06 → 10-13 (7 d 8 h);\n- `ci_runs.tsv.zst`: snapshot of 700 TESSERA CI runs (time, conclusion, event).\n\nLatency is mean push-detect latency, bootstrap 95% CI over pushes, 5 seeded runs (all 5 within ±0.01 of this one).\n\n| Policy | Cron wakes/day | Full (rate-limited) checks/day | Mean latency [95% CI] | Max |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| fixed 5 min (baseline) | 288.1 | 288.1 | 2.51 [2.40, 2.63] min | 5.0 |\n| **ETag 5 min (new default)** | 288.1 | **31.8 (−89%)** | **2.51 [2.40, 2.63]** (identical) | 5.0 |\n| backoff cap 30 (rev 1) | 78.2 | 78.2 | 5.86 [5.40, 6.34] | 28.4 |\n| backoff cap 10 | 158.0 | 158.0 | 3.35 [3.17, 3.54] | 9.8 |\n| backoff after 1 h quiet | 118.8 | 118.8 | 4.77 [4.30, 5.26] | 29.1 |\n| backoff after 4 h quiet | 177.7 | 177.7 | 4.14 [3.74, 4.57] | 28.0 |\n\n**Limits:**\n- Cron wakes (Actions minutes) are not reduced; only full checks and API quota are.\n- Every wake-reducing setting gives a latency CI that doesn't overlap the baseline's.\n- This is a simulation on real timestamps, and assumes 304s are free per GitHub's conditional-request docs.\n- It is not wired into any workflow; that would touch locked paths.\n- It is related to #295 (that PR caches PR-list ETags; this one gates the cron's full check on the head ref).\n\nTests: `python3 -m unittest test_quiet_backoff` (8 tests, including a replay-reproduction CURSOR_AGENT_PR_BODY_BEGIN -->\nSecurity FAIL on merge `efcca741` (PR #64): GATE_RULES §5 told an agent that APPROVE means it may merge. The queue scripts also honored `--merge` (`gh pr merge`) and `--father-approved` (skip protected paths, flag defaults, and gate-tamper checks). This retires those legacy bypasses. It does not merge.\n\n## What changed\n\n- **GATE_RULES stays a draft.** Only the §5 clause that said the agent may merge is struck. The live sentence says an APPROVE verdict does not authorize a merge. HOLD and DENY are unchanged.\n- **`gate_check.py` always returns `may_merge: false`**, including on APPROVE.\n- **`autogate.sh` does not merge.** `--merge` and `--father-approved` exit 2 before any fetch. Any other flag exits 2.\n- **`gatecheck.py` has no father-approved bypass.** Those same flags print a fail object and exit 2. Protected paths, `TESSERA_ENABLE_*` edits, and gate references are rejected with no exception.\n- Prompt, README, and the protected-paths note no longer tell agents the script will merge or that a flag can open the immutable core.\n\nNo `TESSERA_ENABLE_*` defaults were flipped. No secrets and no vault paths.\n\n## Tests\n\n`cd mesh/queue && python3 -m unittest tests.test_spawn tests.test_no_self_merge`\n\n18 tests, OK. They show the retired flags are rejected, a `father_approved` argument cannot open a protected path, §5's live text does not say the agent may merge, and an APPROVE verdict has `may_merge` false.\n\n## Review note\n\nThis PR edits protected paths (`autogate.sh`, `gatecheck.py`, the queue tests, the prompt template, and `mesh/policy/protected-paths.txt`). Autogate will reject it. That is expected: the bypass flag is gone, so this does not merge itself.\n\n<!-- CURSOR_AGENT_PR_BODY_END -->\n\n<div><a Draft only — do not merge.\n\nMeasured prototypes land at `mesh/rick-workspace/rick/tools/git72/` (from `RESULTS.md`, all local, tokens=0):\n\n1. **Git as web** (20 pages + CSV): bytes/page tar 2,048 → bundle 192 → bundle.zst 148 (13.8× smaller). Full sync: untar 2–8 ms, clone from bundle 12–27 ms, zst+clone 14–36 ms. A 1-page update as a delta bundle is 374 B, fetch 8–16 ms, verified. KEPT: plain bundle + delta bundles. zstd is optional (−23% size, +~30% time).\n3. **Offline git queue**: 20/20 jobs claimed → fixed → results committed to a local bare hub. 2.1–4.5 s (4.5–9.6 jobs/s), 0 tokens, vs an online stub of 10.0 s / 10,000 tokens. KEPT.\n5. **Disk / autoclean**: dedup was a silent no-op (jdupes skips read-only git objects). Fixture with `hardlink` saved 0 → 57,344 B (6.5%). Box dry run: 786 KiB. KEPT (small). See `disk/RESULTS.md`.\n6. **Read one file**: full clone 17–44 ms / 53.8 KB; blobless+sparse 27–68 ms / 34.0 KB (DROPPED at this size); `git archive` 2.6–7.6 ms / 806 B; `git cat-file -p rev:path` 2.1–4.0 ms / 806 B; `cat` on an existing worktree 0.01 ms / 0 B. Winner: reuse a worktree. With no worktree, `cat-file`/`archive` is ~10× faster and ~67× less disk than a full clone.\n\nItems 2 and 4 are owned by Tessera (Actions local-model loop and the #71 identity gap) and are not in this pack.\n\nThe `/workspace/ops/autoclean.sh` hardlink fix is box-local and is not in this pack. `disk/RESULTS.md` documents it.\n\nBranched from `rick/workspace-save-20261013` (this run’s starting checkout). CURSOR_AGENT_PR_BODY_BEGIN -->\n## Summary\n\n`tools/qlab/test_aqft.py` and `tools/qlab/test_aqft_fix.py` were sweeps with no assertions: a crash-free run was a pass. They are now pytest tests that `aqft.py` must satisfy:\n\n- normalization and unitarity (`|U†U−I|`, statevector norm, `inverse(aqft)` round-trip, and `aqft(n,n)` equal to `qft(n)`)\n- ideal fidelity in `[0, 1]`, non-decreasing in `m`, and 1 at `m=n` (every basis state for n=2..5, plus the seed-5 sample)\n- gate and lowered 2-qubit counts non-decreasing in `m` and bounded by the exact QFT, for n=1..10\n- bit-identical repeats at a fixed seed\n- `examples/aqft5.json` (`[\"AQFT\",[5,4]]`) expands to the drop-rule circuit (16 gates, 9 CP, 24 lowered 2q)\n\nCurrent outputs are frozen in `tools/qlab/baselines/aqft.json`. Regenerate with:\n\n```\nOMP_NUM_THREADS=1 python3 tools/qlab/baselines/measure_aqft.py\n```\n\nThat script reruns the old seeds on a slice that finishes in about 1 s: ideal fidelity seed 5, n=3..6, 8 inputs; noisy fidelity seed 11, n=3..5, 4 inputs, `p1=1e-3`, `p2=1e-2`, `gamma=1e-3`; phase estimation seed 55, t=5, 10 phases, m in {5, 3, 2}. The original n≤10 sweep (484 s) and the n=9,10 fix rerun (part of 327 s) are not recomputed; those rows stay in `RESULTS.md`.\n\n`python3 -m qlab test` now runs both files. `aqft.py` is unchanged.\n\n## Before / after\n\nMeasured on the existing RESULTS.md protocol and on `examples/aqft5.json` (neither sample was constructed for this change). numpy 1.26.4, `OMP_NUM_THREADS=1`. Because `aqft.py` is unchanged, before and after are the same numbers. There is no measurable improvement in approximation quality.\n\n| quantity | RESULTS.md | remeasured (before = after) |\n|---|---|---|\n| ideal F, n=4 m=3, seed 5, 8 inputs | 0.990485 | 0.990485 |\n| ideal F, n=5 m=3 | 0.959550 | 0.959550 |\n| ideal F, n=6 m=3 | 0.906327 | 0.906327 |\n| ideal min, n=5 m=1 | 0.000027 | 0.000027 |\n| noisy F, n=3 m=3, seed 11, 4 inputs | 0.8676 | 0.867631 |\n| noisy F, n=4 m=3 vs m=4 | 0.7609 vs 0.7578 | 0.760872 vs 0.757793 |\n| noisy F, n=5 m=3 vs m=5 | 0.7012 vs 0.6686 | 0.701228 vs 0.668578 |\n| PE t=5 exact ideal / noisy, seed 55, 10 phases | 0.7860±0.1656 / 0.4403±0.0888 | 0.785997±0.165640 / 0.440258±0.088810 |\n| PE t=5 m=3 ideal / noisy | 0.7488±0.1729 / 0.4581±0.1028 | 0.748829±0.172943 / 0.458088±0.102757 |\n| `aqft5.json` \\|0⟩ fidelity vs exact QFT | — | 0.9999999999999996 |\n| lowered 2q for AQFT[5,4] | 24 | 24 |\n| max \\|U†U−I\\|, n=2..4, all m | — | 8.88e-16 |\n\nSuite runtime: 10 passed in 5.3 s (`pytest tools/qlab/test_aqft.py tools/qlab/test_aqft_fix.py`). Inside `python3 -m qlab test`: `test_aqft.py` 0.7 s, `test_aqft_fix.py` 5.0 s. `ALL PASS` for the full qlab suite. Well under 60 s.\n\n## Broken `aqft` (not committed)\n\nReplacing `aqft()` with an inverted cutoff, a non-unitary phase, and an unseeded angle:\n\n```python\ndef aqft(n, m):\n    c = [g for g in qft(n) if not (g[0] == 'CP' and g[2] > 2 * np.pi / 2**m)]\n    c.append(('P', [0], 0.25j + 0.01 * np.random.random()))\n    return c\n```\n\n`pytest tools/qlab/test_aqft.py tools/qlab/test_aqft_fix.py` then reported **10 failed in 4.30 s**, one failure per test:\n\n- `test_normalization_and_unitarity` — max \\|U†U−I\\| was 0.197, not &lt; 1e-9\n- `test_ideal_fidelity_monotonic_and_bounded` — per-input fidelity dipped as m increased\n- `test_gate_counts_monotone_and_bounded_by_qft` — circuit was not the drop-rule subsequence of `qft`\n- `test_deterministic_with_fixed_seed` — two seed-5 runs disagreed (0.791033 vs 0.791022)\n- `test_matches_aqft5_reference` — AQFT[5,4] gate list no longer matched the reference expansion\n- `test_pe_normalization_and_bounds` — inverse-AQFT inside phase estimation was not the spec circuit\n- `test_pe_ideal_ordered_noise_bounded` — exact ideal P(correct) 0.329 fell below the m=3 arm 0.433 (baseline exact is 0.786)\n- `test_noisy_fidelity_bounded` — `aqft(n,n)` was not `qft(n)`\n- `test_deterministic_pe_and_noisy_avg` — repeated noisy fidelities differed\n- `test_matches_fix_baseline` — PE exact ideal mean 0.330 vs baseline 0.786\n\n`aqft.py` was restored afterward and is not part of this diff. Re-run on the restored file: 10 passed.\n\n<!-- CURSOR_AGENT_PR_BODY_END -->\n\n<div><a CURSOR_AGENT_PR_BODY_BEGIN -->\n## What changed\n\nSHEPHERD-RELAY stays design-only. This adds the offline, keyless gate only:\n\n- `tools/shepherd/sources.json` — one allowlist record each for arXiv RSS/Atom, Wikimedia dumps, arXiv OAI-PMH, MedlinePlus XML, PMC OA (`set=pmc-open`), the National Weather Service API, OpenStreetMap planet replication diffs, and GitHub public releases.\n- Each record stores the license, terms URL, and rate limit as published on the cited page. Where the publisher does not publish a number, the record says so.\n- `tools/shepherd/check_terms.py` — rejects a source that lacks a license, a terms URL, or a published rate limit, or that requires auth, carries a credential, or sets a rate-limit-evasion flag.\n- No network fetch, no scraping, no raising a published cap. Bundle signing and encryption are out of scope.\n\nAuthenticated quotas are not stored. Wikimedia Enterprise HTML dumps, NOAA token APIs, and MedlinePlus RSS (personal use only) are excluded in the record notes.\n\n## Tests\n\n`python3 tools/shepherd/test_check_terms.py` on this machine: 13 tests, OK, in 0.006s. The suite blocks `socket.socket.connect` and checks that the module does not import a client. `python3 tools/shepherd/check_terms.py` reports `accepted 8 rejected 0`.\n\n## Before / after\n\nMeasured on Gitbook `main` `bd1b475b678944a5118b524dfd93646da4e4e65b` (same SHA as `origin/main` at start). That tree has no `tools/shepherd`, so the previous machine-checked source count is **0**. After this change the checker accepts **8** allowlisted sources and rejects **0** of them.\n\nThere is **no measurable improvement** on an existing accuracy, recall, or latency number. No prior gate existed, and the rejection cases in the unit tests were written for this change, so they are not an external score.\n\n## Limits\n\nOpen PRs at start did not touch `tools/shepherd` (#45, #40, #39). Not merged.\n<!-- CURSOR_AGENT_PR_BODY_END -->\n\n<div><a href=\"https://cursor.com/agents/bc-9edda2cd-287c-5f58-b49b-b528018b3ce6?cursor_ref=pr_footer&cursor_cta=open_in_web\"><picture><source
